- [ ] **Step 4: Create `air/packages/mcp-trust-cli/src/run.ts`**

```ts
/**
 * The four `air-mcp` operations over the `mcpTrust` service. Every outcome is
 * an exit code; nothing here exits the process or reads the command line.
 * @module
 */

import type { McpTrust, ObservedSurface } from '@air/dsh-mcp-trust'

/** One parsed command. */
export type TrustCliInvocation =
  | { kind: 'pin'; server: string; tools?: string[]; yes: boolean }
  | { kind: 'diff'; server: string }
  | { kind: 'verify'; all: boolean; server?: string }
  | { kind: 'revoke'; server: string; tools?: string[] }

/** Terminal operations; tests substitute them. */
export interface CliIo {
  /**
   * Write one line of results.
   * @param text - line without a trailing newline.
   */
  out(text: string): void
  /**
   * Write one diagnostic line.
   * @param text - line without a trailing newline.
   */
  err(text: string): void
  /**
   * Whether stdin is an interactive terminal.
   * @returns true when a confirmation can be asked.
   */
  isTty(): boolean
  /**
   * Ask a yes/no question.
   * @param question - prompt text.
   * @returns true only for an explicit yes.
   */
  confirm(question: string): Promise<boolean>
}

/** Every named surface is approved, or the write succeeded. */
export const EXIT_OK = 0
/** A surface differs from its pin, or a pin was refused or declined. */
export const EXIT_DRIFT = 1
/** A server was not observed, or an operation failed. */
export const EXIT_ERROR = 2

const TRUSTED: readonly string[] = ['approved', 'tofu']

function drifted(surface: ObservedSurface): boolean {
  return !TRUSTED.includes(surface.state)
}

function missing(server: string, io: CliIo): number {
  io.err(`air-mcp: server "${server}" was not observed. This profile connects only the mcp-client rows in its own profile patch, each with inject: [mcpToolReview]; a server that a project's .mcp.json adds is not connected here. Check that the row exists and that the server starts.`)
  return EXIT_ERROR
}

function diff(trust: McpTrust, server: string, io: CliIo): number {
  const surface = trust.observed(server)
  if (surface === undefined) return missing(server, io)
  io.out(trust.describe(server))
  return drifted(surface) ? EXIT_DRIFT : EXIT_OK
}

function verify(trust: McpTrust, invocation: { all: boolean; server?: string }, io: CliIo): number {
  if (!invocation.all && invocation.server === undefined) {
    io.err('air-mcp: verify needs a server name or --all')
    return EXIT_ERROR
  }
  const names = invocation.server === undefined ? trust.servers() : [invocation.server]
  if (names.length === 0) io.out('no MCP servers were observed')
  let code = EXIT_OK
  for (const name of names) {
    const surface = trust.observed(name)
    if (surface === undefined) {
      code = missing(name, io)
      continue
    }
    io.out(`${name}: ${surface.state}`)
    if (!drifted(surface)) continue
    io.out(trust.describe(name))
    code = Math.max(code, EXIT_DRIFT)
  }
  return code
}

async function pin(
  trust: McpTrust,
  invocation: { server: string; tools?: string[]; yes: boolean },
  io: CliIo,
): Promise<number> {
  const { server } = invocation
  if (trust.observed(server) === undefined) return missing(server, io)
  io.out(trust.describe(server))
  if (!invocation.yes) {
    if (!io.isTty()) {
      io.err('air-mcp: refusing to pin without --yes because stdin is not a terminal')
      return EXIT_DRIFT
    }
    if (!await io.confirm(`Pin this surface for "${server}"? [y/N] `)) {
      io.err('air-mcp: not pinned')
      return EXIT_DRIFT
    }
  }
  await trust.pin(server, { ...invocation.tools === undefined ? {} : { tools: invocation.tools }, approvedBy: 'cli' })
  io.out(`pinned ${server}`)
  return EXIT_OK
}

async function revoke(trust: McpTrust, invocation: { server: string; tools?: string[] }, io: CliIo): Promise<number> {
  await trust.revoke(invocation.server, invocation.tools === undefined ? {} : { tools: invocation.tools })
  io.out(invocation.tools === undefined ? `revoked ${invocation.server}` : `revoked ${invocation.server}: ${invocation.tools.join(', ')}`)
  return EXIT_OK
}

/**
 * Run one command.
 * @param trust - the trust service of the booted tree.
 * @param invocation - parsed command.
 * @param io - terminal operations.
 * @returns the process exit code; failures are reported through `io.err` and never thrown.
 */
export async function runTrustCli(trust: McpTrust, invocation: TrustCliInvocation, io: CliIo): Promise<number> {
  try {
    switch (invocation.kind) {
      case 'diff': return diff(trust, invocation.server, io)
      case 'verify': return verify(trust, invocation, io)
      case 'pin': return await pin(trust, invocation, io)
      case 'revoke': return await revoke(trust, invocation, io)
    }
  } catch (error) {
    io.err(`air-mcp: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`)
    return EXIT_ERROR
  }
}
```

The `switch` has no `default`: `TrustCliInvocation` is a closed union, and the declared `Promise<number>` return type makes `tsc` reject a missing case.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the failing test `air/packages/mcp-trust-cli/tests/startup.spec.ts`**

The provider boots through the real Loader with real `dsh-cmdline` parsing; the trust service and the launcher facts are stand-ins.

```ts
/** The air-mcp command-line provider over a real Loader tree: one test per subcommand plus help, usage, and pending cases. */
import { mkdtempSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import { join } from 'node:path'
import { PassThrough } from 'node:stream'
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url'
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest'
import { Context } from '@deepseek-ai/cordis'
import Include from '@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-include'
import Loader from '@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-loader'
import { internals as cmdlineInternals, provideCmdline } from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-cmdline'
import type { McpTrust, ObservedSurface } from '@air/dsh-mcp-trust'
import * as cli from '../src/index.ts'
import { internals } from '../src/internals.ts'

declare global {
  // eslint-disable-next-line no-var -- a global binding is the only channel into natively imported rows
  var __airMcpTrustCli: { apply: typeof cli.apply; trust: McpTrust } | undefined
}

const original = { ...internals }
const disposers: (() => Promise<void>)[] = []
const dirs: string[] = []

afterEach(async () => {
  for (const dispose of disposers.splice(0)) await dispose()
  for (const dir of dirs.splice(0)) rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true })
  Object.assign(internals, original)
  cmdlineInternals.stdout = process.stdout
  cmdlineInternals.stderr = process.stderr
})

const APPROVED: ObservedSurface = {
  serverName: 'browser', surfaceDigest: 'sha256:1', instructions: '', tools: [],
  diff: { added: [], removed: [], changed: [], instructionsChanged: false },
  state: 'approved', action: 'accept', withheld: [], observedAt: 0,
}

function fakeTrust(surfaces: ObservedSurface[]) {
  const byName = new Map(surfaces.map(each => [each.serverName, each] as const))
  return {
    observed: vi.fn((name: string) => byName.get(name)),
    servers: vi.fn(() => [...byName.keys()]),
    describe: vi.fn((name: string) => `server ${name}: ${byName.get(name)?.state ?? 'missing'}`),
    pin: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve()),
    revoke: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve()),
    verify: vi.fn(() => surfaces),
  } satisfies McpTrust
}

async function bootCli(args: string[], options: { surfaces?: ObservedSurface[]; withTrust?: boolean } = {}) {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'air-mcp-cli-'))
  dirs.push(dir)
  const trust = fakeTrust(options.surfaces ?? [APPROVED])
  globalThis.__airMcpTrustCli = { apply: cli.apply, trust }
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'cli.mjs'), [
    'export const name = \'air-mcp-trust-cli\'',
    'export const inject = [\'cmdlineArgs\', \'mcpTrust\']',
    'export const apply = ctx => globalThis.__airMcpTrustCli.apply(ctx)',
    '',
  ].join('\n'))
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'trust.mjs'), [
    'export const name = \'fake-mcp-trust\'',
    'export const apply = (ctx) => { ctx.provide(\'mcpTrust\', globalThis.__airMcpTrustCli.trust) }',
    '',
  ].join('\n'))
  const rows = [
    { id: 'air-mcp-trust-cli', name: pathToFileURL(join(dir, 'cli.mjs')).href },
    ...options.withTrust === false ? [] : [{ id: 'air-mcp-trust', name: pathToFileURL(join(dir, 'trust.mjs')).href }],
  ]
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'cordis.yml'), JSON.stringify(rows))

  const out: string[] = []
  const err: string[] = []
  const exits: number[] = []
  internals.stdout = { write: (chunk: string) => out.push(chunk) }
  internals.stderr = { write: (chunk: string) => err.push(chunk) }
  cmdlineInternals.stdout = { write: (chunk: string) => out.push(chunk) }
  cmdlineInternals.stderr = { write: (chunk: string) => err.push(chunk) }

  const listeners = new Set<() => void>()
  const ctx = new Context()
  await ctx.plugin(Loader)
  ctx.loader.builtins.include = Include
  provideCmdline(ctx, {
    args,
    exit: code => void exits.push(code),
    ready: {
      onReady(listener) {
        listeners.add(listener)
        return () => { listeners.delete(listener) }
      },
    },
  })
  await ctx.loader.create({ name: 'cordis:include', config: { path: pathToFileURL(join(dir, 'cordis.yml')).href } })
  await ctx.loader.await()
  let disposed: Promise<void> | undefined
  const dispose = (): Promise<void> => disposed ??= Promise.resolve(ctx.fiber.dispose()).then(() => {})
  disposers.push(dispose)
  const commit = (): void => { for (const listener of listeners) listener() }
  return { trust, out, err, exits, listeners, commit, dispose }
}

describe('air-mcp command-line provider', () => {
  it('verify --all runs after startup commits and exits 0 on a match', async () => {
    const { out, exits, commit } = await bootCli(['verify', '--all'])
    expect(exits).toEqual([])
    commit()
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([0]) })
    expect(out).toEqual(['browser: approved\n'])
  })

  it('verify <server> exits 1 on drift', async () => {
    const { exits, commit } = await bootCli(['verify', 'browser'], { surfaces: [{ ...APPROVED, state: 'quarantined' }] })
    commit()
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([1]) })
  })

  it('diff exits 2 for a server that was not observed and reports on stderr', async () => {
    const { err, exits, commit } = await bootCli(['diff', 'ghost'])
    commit()
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([2]) })
    expect(err.join('')).toContain('server "ghost" was not observed')
  })

  it('pin --yes --tool passes the selection and exits 0', async () => {
    const { trust, exits, commit } = await bootCli(['pin', 'browser', '--tool', 'nav', 'snap', '--yes'])
    commit()
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([0]) })
    expect(trust.pin).toHaveBeenCalledWith('browser', { tools: ['nav', 'snap'], approvedBy: 'cli' })
  })

  it('pin without --yes refuses on a non-terminal stdin', async () => {
    internals.stdin = new PassThrough()
    const { trust, exits, commit } = await bootCli(['pin', 'browser'])
    commit()
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([1]) })
    expect(trust.pin).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })

  it('pin without --yes asks on a terminal and accepts a typed yes', async () => {
    const stdin = Object.assign(new PassThrough(), { isTTY: true })
    internals.stdin = stdin
    const { trust, err, exits, commit } = await bootCli(['pin', 'browser'])
    commit()
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(err.join('')).toContain('Pin this surface for "browser"? [y/N] ') })
    stdin.write('Yes\n')
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([0]) })
    expect(trust.pin).toHaveBeenCalledWith('browser', { approvedBy: 'cli' })
  })

  it('pin without --yes treats any other answer as no', async () => {
    const stdin = Object.assign(new PassThrough(), { isTTY: true })
    internals.stdin = stdin
    const { trust, exits, commit } = await bootCli(['pin', 'browser'])
    commit()
    stdin.write('maybe\n')
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([1]) })
    expect(trust.pin).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })

  it('revoke --tool passes the selection and exits 0', async () => {
    const { trust, exits, commit } = await bootCli(['revoke', 'browser', '--tool', 'nav'])
    commit()
    await vi.waitFor(() => { expect(exits).toEqual([0]) })
    expect(trust.revoke).toHaveBeenCalledWith('browser', { tools: ['nav'] })
  })

  it('--help prints usage, exits 0, and runs nothing', async () => {
    const { out, exits, listeners } = await bootCli(['--help'])
    expect(exits).toEqual([0])
    expect(out.join('')).toContain('Usage: dsh --profile air-mcp')
    expect(out.join('')).toContain('pin')
    expect(listeners.size).toBe(0)
  })

  it('an unknown command is a usage error', async () => {
    const { err, exits, listeners } = await bootCli(['approve', 'browser'])
    expect(exits).toEqual([1])
    expect(err.join('')).toContain('unknown command')
    expect(listeners.size).toBe(0)
  })

  it('stays pending without the trust service and cancels its startup listener on unload', async () => {
    const pending = await bootCli(['verify', '--all'], { withTrust: false })
    expect(pending.listeners.size).toBe(0)
    expect(pending.exits).toEqual([])

    const loaded = await bootCli(['verify', '--all'])
    expect(loaded.listeners.size).toBe(1)
    await loaded.dispose()
    expect(loaded.listeners.size).toBe(0)
  })

  it('fails loud when the launcher facts or the trust service are absent', () => {
    const bare = new Context()
    expect(() => { cli.apply(bare) }).toThrow('the launcher must provide ctx.appReady and ctx.appExit, and the tree must provide mcpTrust')
    provideCmdline(bare, { args: [], exit: () => {} })
    expect(() => { cli.apply(bare) }).toThrow('the launcher must provide')
    const noTrust = new Context()
    provideCmdline(noTrust, { args: [], exit: () => {}, ready: { onReady: () => () => {} } })
    expect(() => { cli.apply(noTrust) }).toThrow('the tree must provide mcpTrust')
  })
})
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Create `air/packages/mcp-trust-cli/src/internals.ts`**

```ts
/** Process streams used by the air-mcp command line; tests substitute them. @module */

/** Replaceable process facts. */
export const internals: {
  stdin: NodeJS.ReadableStream & { isTTY?: boolean }
  stdout: { write(chunk: string): unknown }
  stderr: { write(chunk: string): unknown }
} = {
  stdin: process.stdin,
  stdout: process.stdout,
  stderr: process.stderr,
}
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Create `air/packages/mcp-trust-cli/src/index.ts`**

```ts
/**
 * Command-line provider of the `air-mcp` profile. It parses
 * `pin | diff | verify | revoke`, waits for the launcher's successful-startup
 * signal (every mcp-client row has finished its first review by then), runs
 * the command against the `mcpTrust` service, and requests process exit with
 * the command's code.
 *
 * Function plugin: named exports only.
 * @module @air/dsh-mcp-trust-cli
 */

import { createInterface } from 'node:readline/promises'
import { Command } from 'commander'
import type { Context } from '@deepseek-ai/cordis'
import { parseCmdline } from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-cmdline'
import type {} from '@air/dsh-mcp-trust'
import { internals } from './internals.ts'
import { runTrustCli, type CliIo, type TrustCliInvocation } from './run.ts'

/** Cordis plugin name used by Loader diagnostics. */
export const name = 'air-mcp-trust-cli'

/** Services required before a command can run. */
export const inject = ['cmdlineArgs', 'mcpTrust']

/**
 * This app's command tree.
 * @param accept - receives the parsed command from the invoked subcommand's action.
 * @returns a fresh program.
 */
function trustCommand(accept: (invocation: TrustCliInvocation) => void): Command {
  const program = new Command()
    .name('dsh --profile air-mcp')
    .description('Review and approve the tool definitions of configured MCP servers.')
    .helpOption('-h, --help', 'show this help')
  program.command('pin <server>')
    .description('approve the server\'s current tool surface in the lockfile')
    .option('--tool <name...>', 'approve only these raw tool names')
    .option('--yes', 'write without asking')
    .action((server: string, options: { tool?: string[]; yes?: boolean }) => {
      accept({ kind: 'pin', server, ...options.tool === undefined ? {} : { tools: options.tool }, yes: options.yes === true })
    })
  program.command('diff <server>')
    .description('show how the server\'s current surface differs from its pin; writes nothing')
    .action((server: string) => { accept({ kind: 'diff', server }) })
  program.command('verify [server]')
    .description('exit non-zero when a surface differs from its pin; writes nothing')
    .option('--all', 'verify every configured server')
    .action((server: string | undefined, options: { all?: boolean }) => {
      accept({ kind: 'verify', all: options.all === true, ...server === undefined ? {} : { server } })
    })
  program.command('revoke <server>')
    .description('remove approvals from the lockfile; running processes deny the tools at once')
    .option('--tool <name...>', 'revoke only these raw tool names')
    .action((server: string, options: { tool?: string[] }) => {
      accept({ kind: 'revoke', server, ...options.tool === undefined ? {} : { tools: options.tool } })
    })
  return program
}

/** Terminal operations over the replaceable process streams. */
const io: CliIo = {
  out: (text) => { internals.stdout.write(`${text}\n`) },
  err: (text) => { internals.stderr.write(`${text}\n`) },
  isTty: () => internals.stdin.isTTY === true,
  async confirm(question) {
    const readline = createInterface({ input: internals.stdin })
    internals.stderr.write(question)
    try {
      return /^y(es)?$/i.test((await readline.question('')).trim())
    } finally {
      readline.close()
    }
  },
}

/**
 * Parse the command line and schedule the command after successful startup.
 * Help and usage errors request exit through `parseCmdline` and schedule nothing.
 * @param ctx - plugin context carrying the command line and the trust service.
 * @throws when the launcher did not provide readiness and exit, or no trust service exists.
 */
export function apply(ctx: Context): void {
  const ready = ctx.get('appReady')
  const exit = ctx.get('appExit')
  const trust = ctx.get('mcpTrust')
  if (ready === undefined || exit === undefined || trust === undefined) {
    throw new Error('air-mcp: the launcher must provide ctx.appReady and ctx.appExit, and the tree must provide mcpTrust')
  }
  let invocation: TrustCliInvocation | undefined
  parseCmdline(ctx, trustCommand((value) => { invocation = value }))
  if (invocation === undefined) return
  const accepted = invocation
  ctx.effect(() => ready.onReady(() => {
    void runTrustCli(trust, accepted, io).then(exit)
  }), 'air-mcp-trust-cli.run')
}
```

One note for the implementer: if `tsc` narrows `invocation` to `undefined` after `parseCmdline` (assignment inside a callback), declare `const holder: { invocation?: TrustCliInvocation } = {}` instead and assign `holder.invocation` in the callback; behavior is unchanged.

- [ ] **Step 12: Write `air/packages/mcp-trust-cli/README.md`**

````markdown
# @air/dsh-mcp-trust-cli

## Summary

Command-line provider of the `air-mcp` profile. It connects the MCP servers whose mcp-client rows are in its own profile patch, compares what each server presents with the lockfile it shares with the `air` profile, and lets a person approve or revoke tool definitions outside any agent session. It is a `dsh` profile, not a package bin. It works the same in PowerShell, cmd, and POSIX shells.

```sh
pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp diff browser                 # field-level difference; writes nothing
pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp pin browser                  # asks before writing; --yes skips the question
pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp pin browser --tool browser_navigate browser_snapshot
pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp verify --all                 # for CI; writes nothing
pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp revoke browser --tool browser_evaluate
```

| Exit code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | Every named surface is approved (or pinned on first use), or the write succeeded |
| 1 | A surface differs from its pin; a pin was refused or declined; a usage error |
| 2 | A server was not observed (not configured, or its connection failed), or an operation failed |

`pin` writes what the process fetched during startup, including tools the review withheld. `revoke` without `--tool` leaves an empty entry for the server, so `tofu` mode cannot pin it again without a person. A running `air` process watches the lockfile and applies a revocation without a restart.

Create the profile once per harness home (the first command prints the composed configuration and can be ignored):

```sh
pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp --from-default-profile headless --dump-config
pnpm dsh plugin --profile air-mcp add "$PWD/air/bundles/mcp-servers"
pnpm dsh plugin --profile air-mcp add "$PWD/air/bundles/air-mcp"
```

## Model Experience

None. This profile runs no agent and sends no model request. Its effect on a model is indirect: a pin or revocation changes which MCP tools the `air` profile registers on its next sync.

## Known Limitations

- `pin` approves the surface this process observed. A server that presents different surfaces to different clients or at different times must be verified again from the `air` profile's own process (its audit log records the digest it ran against).
- Server rows live in each profile's own patch. A server defined only in the `air` profile, or one that a project's `.mcp.json` adds (plan 01), is not connected here, so `diff` and `pin` report it as not observed. For those servers the approval prompt in the `air` profile is the way to save a first approval; a later change to such a server is accepted for one process at a time. A single shared server list is a follow-up (see the plan).
- If the lockfile cannot be read, every command reports the path and the parse error and changes nothing; repair the file or move it aside and pin each server again.
- The profile starts every configured MCP server in order to read its surface. Servers with side effects at startup run them here as well.
- Output is plain text. There is no machine-readable format; CI should rely on exit codes.
- Server instructions are approved only by a whole-surface `pin`; `pin --tool` keeps the previous instruction pin.
````

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test `air/packages/mcp-trust/tests/bundle.spec.ts`**

```ts
/** AIR bundle and example patches: the trust row exists, and every mcp-client row is reviewed. */
import { readdirSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { join } from 'node:path'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import * as yaml from 'js-yaml'
import { entryListSchema } from '@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-include'
import { resolvePolicy } from '../src/verdict.ts'

const air = join(import.meta.dirname, '..', '..', '..')

interface Row {
  id?: string
  name?: string
  inject?: string[]
  disabled?: boolean
  config?: Record<string, unknown>
  insert?: Row[]
}

function rows(file: string): Row[] {
  const parsed = yaml.load(readFileSync(file, 'utf8'), { schema: entryListSchema }) as Row[]
  return parsed.flatMap(row => row.insert ?? [row])
}

const patches = [
  join(air, 'bundles', 'air', 'cordis.patch.yml'),
  join(air, 'bundles', 'mcp-servers', 'cordis.patch.yml'),
  join(air, 'bundles', 'air-mcp', 'cordis.patch.yml'),
  ...readdirSync(join(air, 'examples')).filter(file => file.endsWith('.yml')).map(file => join(air, 'examples', file)),
]

describe('AIR MCP composition', () => {
  it('ships the trust row with a complete fail-closed default policy', () => {
    const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(air, 'bundles', 'mcp-servers', 'package.json'), 'utf8')) as {
      name: string
      dependencies: Record<string, string>
      dsh: { bundle: { patch: string } }
    }
    expect(manifest.name).toBe('@air/dsh-air-mcp-servers')
    expect(manifest.dsh.bundle.patch).toBe('./cordis.patch.yml')
    expect(manifest.dependencies['@air/dsh-mcp-trust']).toBe('workspace:*')

    const trust = rows(join(air, 'bundles', 'mcp-servers', 'cordis.patch.yml')).find(row => row.id === 'air-mcp-trust')
    expect(trust?.name).toBe('@air/dsh-mcp-trust')
    expect(resolvePolicy(trust?.config?.['defaults'], undefined, 'defaults')).toEqual({
      mode: 'enforce', onAdded: 'withhold', onChanged: 'reject-generation', onRemoved: 'accept',
      instructions: 'pin', deny: [], override: {},
    })
    expect(trust?.config?.['denyUnreviewedMcpTools']).toBe(true)
    expect(trust?.config?.['cliCommand']).toBe('pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp')
    expect(trust?.config?.['enrollOnApproval']).toBe(true)
  })

  it('makes the air bundle hold every .mcp.json server until the reviewer exists (plan 01 switch)', () => {
    const conventions = rows(join(air, 'bundles', 'air', 'cordis.patch.yml')).find(row => row.id === 'air-mcp-conventions')
    expect(conventions?.name).toBe('@air/dsh-mcp-conventions')
    expect(conventions?.config).toEqual({ reviewTools: true })
  })

  it('declares inject: [mcpToolReview] on every mcp-client row (scenario 23)', () => {
    const clients = patches.flatMap(file => rows(file)
      .filter(row => row.name === '@deepseek-ai/dsh-mcp-client')
      .map(row => ({ file, id: row.id, inject: row.inject ?? [] })))
    expect(clients.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)
    expect(clients.filter(row => !row.inject.includes('mcpToolReview'))).toEqual([])
  })

  it('turns off the headless rows and inserts the command line in the air-mcp bundle', () => {
    const patch = rows(join(air, 'bundles', 'air-mcp', 'cordis.patch.yml'))
    expect(patch.filter(row => row.disabled === true).map(row => row.id)).toEqual(['headless-startup', 'headless-runner'])
    expect(patch.find(row => row.id === 'air-mcp-trust-cli')?.name).toBe('@air/dsh-mcp-trust-cli')
  })
})
```

`air/bundles/mcp-servers/cordis.patch.yml`:

```yaml
# Shared MCP composition, listed by both the `air` and the `air-mcp` profile so
# they connect the same servers and read one lockfile.
#
# Rule (checked by air/packages/mcp-trust/tests/bundle.spec.ts): every
# mcp-client row in an AIR patch declares `inject: [mcpToolReview]`. The row
# then stays pending until the trust plugin exists, so no tool definition can
# reach a model unreviewed.

# Base-layer rows that send data to DeepSeek services or need a DeepSeek
# account. The `air` bundle disables them too; repeating them here keeps the
# `air-mcp` profile, which does not list the `air` bundle, from contacting
# those services. Web-only rows stay in the `air` bundle.
- id: session-log-deepseek
  disabled: true

- id: deepseek-account
  disabled: true

- id: llm-deepseek-account
  disabled: true

- id: web-search-deepseek
  disabled: true

- insert:
    - id: air-mcp-trust
      name: '@air/dsh-mcp-trust'
      config:
        # One file for every profile under this harness home. Point it at a
        # path inside a repository to review pins as diffs.
        lockfile: !!js dshHomePath('air', 'mcp-lock.json')
        auditDir: !!js dshHomePath('air', 'mcp-audit')
        defaults:
          mode: enforce
          onAdded: withhold
          onChanged: reject-generation
          onRemoved: accept
          instructions: pin
        denyUnreviewedMcpTools: true
        maxPromptsPerServer: 2
        minReverifyMs: 60000
        maxReverifyMs: 86400000
        lockWaitMs: 2000
        watchDebounceMs: 200
        # Named in every prompt and denial. Replace it where the profile is
        # launched differently (a packaged app, for example).
        cliCommand: pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp
        # First use: approving a server that has no lock entry saves the pin.
        # A server whose pinned surface changed is never pinned by a prompt.
        enrollOnApproval: true
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Create the example `air/examples/mcp-filesystem.profile.cordis.patch.yml`**

```yaml
# Example profile patch: one reviewed MCP server. Copy the row into
# $DSH_HOME/profiles/<profile>/cordis.patch.yml of a profile that lists
# @air/dsh-air-mcp-servers and can resolve @deepseek-ai/dsh-mcp-client (see
# that package's README, "Minimal configuration").
#
# `inject: [mcpToolReview]` is required on every mcp-client row. The launch
# arguments name an exact package version, because the lockfile pins the tool
# definitions and not the code that serves them.
- insert:
    - id: mcp-files
      name: '@deepseek-ai/dsh-mcp-client'
      inject: [mcpToolReview]
      config:
        transport: stdio
        serverName: files
        command: npx
        args: ['-y', '@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem@2026.7.10', '.']
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Create `air/scripts/smoke-air-mcp.ts`**

The script runs on Windows and Linux: it starts the launcher with `process.execPath`, passes arguments as an array (no shell), and reads output through Node, not through redirects or `grep`.

```ts
/** Compose the air-mcp profile under an isolated DSH_HOME and check the exit codes of its commands (Windows and Linux). */
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process'
import { existsSync, mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path'

const repoRoot = resolve(import.meta.dirname, '..', '..')
const home = process.env.DSH_HOME ?? mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'air-smoke-mcp-'))
const timeoutMs = Number(process.env.AIR_SMOKE_SECONDS ?? '120') * 1000
const profile = 'air-mcp'
const env = { ...process.env, DSH_HOME: home }
const launcher = ['--import', 'tsx/esm', join(repoRoot, 'apps', 'cli', 'src', 'bin.ts')]
const PROBLEM = /unmatched|incompatible|failed/i

function fail(message: string, detail = ''): never {
  console.error(`smoke-air-mcp: ${message}`)
  if (detail !== '') console.error(detail)
  process.exit(1)
}

/** Run the launcher and return its exit code and text; a timeout reports status `null`. */
function dsh(args: readonly string[]): { status: number | null, stdout: string, stderr: string } {
  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, [...launcher, ...args], { cwd: repoRoot, env, encoding: 'utf8', timeout: timeoutMs })
  return { status: result.status, stdout: result.stdout, stderr: result.stderr }
}

/** Run one command of the profile and require its exit code. */
function expectExit(want: number, args: readonly string[]): { stdout: string, stderr: string } {
  const result = dsh(['--profile', profile, ...args])
  if (result.status !== want) fail(`'${args.join(' ')}' exited ${String(result.status)}, expected ${String(want)}`, result.stderr)
  return result
}

const created = dsh(['--profile', profile, '--from-default-profile', 'headless', '--dump-config'])
if (created.status !== 0) fail('profile creation failed', created.stderr)
for (const bundle of ['mcp-servers', 'air-mcp']) {
  const added = dsh(['plugin', '--profile', profile, 'add', join(repoRoot, 'air', 'bundles', bundle)])
  if (added.status !== 0) fail(`adding bundle ${bundle} failed`, added.stderr)
}

const dump = expectExit(0, ['--dump-config'])
if (PROBLEM.test(dump.stderr)) fail('composition problems:', dump.stderr)
if (!dump.stdout.includes('air-mcp-trust-cli')) fail('command-line row missing')
if (!dump.stdout.includes('air-mcp-trust')) fail('trust row missing')

if (!expectExit(0, ['--help']).stdout.includes('revoke')) fail('help text missing')
if (!expectExit(0, ['verify', '--all']).stdout.includes('no MCP servers were observed')) fail('verify output missing')
if (!expectExit(2, ['diff', 'ghost']).stderr.includes('was not observed')) fail('diff diagnostic missing')
expectExit(2, ['pin', 'ghost', '--yes'])

if (existsSync(join(home, 'air', 'mcp-lock.json'))) fail('a read-only command wrote the lockfile')
console.log(`smoke-air-mcp: ok (${home})`)
```

No MCP server is configured here; server-backed behavior is covered by the package tests, whose fixture server starts with `process.execPath` and so runs on Windows too.

Run: `pnpm -C air exec tsx scripts/smoke-air-mcp.ts`
Expected: last line `smoke-air-mcp: ok (...)`; nothing written under the user's `.dsh` directory. Diagnose by symptom:
- `'--help' exited null`: the run hit the timeout, so the tree did not request exit; check the printed stderr for a pending row. A pending `air-mcp-trust-cli` row means `mcpTrust` was not provided: confirm `@air/dsh-air-mcp-servers` is listed in `profiles/air-mcp/package.json` under the isolated home and that `pnpm -C air run build` ran.
- `'verify --all' exited 1` with a headless task error: the two headless rows are still active; confirm the bundle order in the profile manifest ends with `@air/dsh-air-mcp-bundle`.
- A composition problem naming one of the four disabled rows as unmatched: that row is not in `dsh-base` at this upstream version; remove it from `air/bundles/mcp-servers/cordis.patch.yml` and keep it only in the `air` bundle.

Then add the servers bundle to the developer's own `air` profile once: `pnpm dsh plugin --profile air add "$PWD/air/bundles/mcp-servers"` (a restart applies it; `"$PWD/..."` works in PowerShell and POSIX shells).

- [ ] **Step 10: Write `air/packages/mcp-trust/README.md`**

````markdown
# @air/dsh-mcp-trust

## Summary

Pins the tool definitions and instructions of MCP servers to a reviewed lockfile. On every tool-list fetch, `dsh-mcp-client` offers the fetched generation to this plugin's `mcpToolReview` service before anything registers. The plugin digests each definition (RFC 8785 canonical JSON, SHA-256), compares it with the lockfile, and returns only the approved tools. A changed definition, by default, removes every tool and the instruction section of that server until a person approves the new surface.

Services: `mcpToolReview` (consumed by mcp-client rows that declare `inject: [mcpToolReview]`) and `mcpTrust` (inspection, `pin`, `revoke`; consumed by `@air/dsh-mcp-trust-cli`).

### Configuration

Every field is required except `servers`. Shipped values live in `air/bundles/mcp-servers/cordis.patch.yml`.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `lockfile` | Absolute path of the lockfile. All profiles that must agree on pins use one path. |
| `auditDir` | Absolute directory for JSON Lines audit files. |
| `defaults` | Policy for every server: `mode` (`off`, `tofu`, `enforce`), `onAdded` (`withhold`, `reject-generation`), `onChanged` (`reject-generation`, `withhold`), `onRemoved` (`accept`, `reject-generation`), `instructions` (`pin`, `drop`, `accept`), optional `allow`, `deny`, `override`. |
| `servers` | Per-server policy overrides keyed by the local `serverName`. |
| `denyUnreviewedMcpTools` | Deny calls to `mcp__` tools that no review registered. |
| `maxPromptsPerServer` | One-shot approval prompts per server per process. |
| `minReverifyMs`, `maxReverifyMs` | Bounds of the re-verification delay taken from a server's `ttlMs`. |
| `lockWaitMs` | Longest wait for the lockfile writer lock. |
| `watchDebounceMs` | Quiet time after a lockfile change before it is re-read. |
| `cliCommand` | Command prefix of the `air-mcp` profile (shipped: `pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp`). Every prompt and denial names the exact `diff`, `pin`, and `revoke` commands under this prefix. |
| `enrollOnApproval` | Approving a server that has no lock entry also saves its pin (shipped: `true`). A server whose pinned surface changed is never pinned by a prompt. |

### Product default: `enforce`, with first use through the prompt

The shipped mode is `enforce`. `tofu` would pin whatever a new server presents without anyone looking, which is the one moment an attacker-controlled description is most dangerous, and it is also the mode a server swap after a long benign history does not defeat. The cost of `enforce` is a first-use step, and the plugin keeps that step to one answer: the first turn after a server appears asks "MCP server `x` has not been approved yet. It offers N tools: ... Approve to use it and remember the approval in the trust lockfile." Allowing it pins the server (`approvedBy: "prompt"`); declining leaves it withheld, and the prompt names `diff <server>` for reading the definitions first. For a development server you trust without review, set `servers: { <name>: { mode: tofu } }` in the trust row; that is an explicit per-server choice and shows in the profile patch.

### What each difference does

| Difference from the pin | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| Tool added | `withhold` | The new tool does not register; the others stay |
| Pinned tool or instructions changed (any digest field) | `reject-generation` | No tool of the server registers and its instruction section is withdrawn |
| Pinned tool removed | `accept` | Logged |
| Definition that is not I-JSON (for example a lone surrogate) | always rejected | Same as a change |
| No pin, `enforce` | | Nothing registers until the first-use prompt is allowed or `pin` runs |
| No pin, `tofu` | | The first surface is pinned with `approvedBy: "tofu"`; later changes are treated as above |

The tool digest covers `name`, `title`, `description`, `inputSchema`, `outputSchema`, `annotations`, and `execution`; `icons` and `_meta` are excluded. Text is never Unicode-normalized, so an invisible or look-alike character is a change. Pins are keyed by the local `serverName`, never by the name a server reports.

### Approving a surface

- First use, from the session: when an approval answerer is composed, the first step of the next turn asks once per server (`maxPromptsPerServer`). For a server with no lock entry, allowing it pins the surface (`enrollOnApproval`). Tools appear from the next step.
- A changed surface, from the session: allowing the prompt accepts exactly that surface digest until the process exits and writes nothing, so the next process asks again.
- Persistent, from a terminal: `pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp pin <server>` after `diff <server>` (see `@air/dsh-mcp-trust-cli`). Running processes watch the lockfile and re-sync.

### How a person finds out that a server is blocked

| Situation | What the person sees |
|---|---|
| Approval service present (default `ask` policy) | The prompt at the first step of the next turn, with the server, what differs, what allowing does, and the `diff` and `pin` commands |
| No approval service, or policy `never` (Full access, headless) | One warning in the harness log per review that found a difference, ending with ``Run `<cli> diff <server>` to review it.``; the server stays withheld. The desktop shell should list `ctx.mcpTrust.servers()` and `observed(server).state` (plan 07 follow-up) |
| The model calls a tool of a blocked server | The tool result is a denial that names the server, the changed fields, and the commands (see Model Experience) |
| The lockfile cannot be read | An error in the log, every MCP tool denied with the lockfile path, `verify --all` exits 1 with the parse error, and the file is left untouched |

### Order with plan 03's per-call ask

Two separate decisions apply to an MCP tool call. Server trust is decided first, when the server's tools are offered: only approved tools register, so a call to a withheld tool cannot start. The per-call ask of `@air/dsh-permission-rules` runs at `tools/pre-execute`, before the guard of this plugin, so for a tool whose approval was revoked after it registered the person may be asked and then see this plugin's denial. An allow rule from plan 03 removes that ask and nothing else; it never approves a server, never changes a lock entry, and never answers this plugin's prompt, because the prompt carries no `callId` and plan 03 answers only asks that belong to a call it saw. Per-server trust levels that skip the per-call ask are a plan 03 follow-up.

### Files

| Path | Content |
|---|---|
| `lockfile` | `{ version: 1, canonicalization: "RFC8785-JCS/SHA-256", servers: { <serverName>: { surfaceDigest, instructions?: { digest, text }, tools: { <rawName>: { digest, fields, definition, approvedAt, approvedBy } } } } }` |
| `auditDir/process.jsonl` | One `mcp/drift` record per review that found a difference: `{ time, type, data: { serverName, added, removed, changed, instructionsChanged, invalid?, action } }` |
| `auditDir/<sessionId>.jsonl` | Per session: an `mcp/surface` record `{ sessionId, turn, time, type, data: { serverName, surfaceDigest, verdict, withheld, lockfileDigest? } }` when a server's digest or state changes, followed by an `mcp/drift` record when that surface differs from its pin |

The record `type` strings are labels inside these files. This plugin appends nothing to session logs.

## Model Experience

### Tool list

#### What the model sees

Only approved MCP tools appear in the tool list. A withheld or rejected tool is absent; the model is not told that it exists. With `override`, the model sees the configured description instead of the server's text.

#### Token effect

Never more tokens than without the plugin: tools are removed or their descriptions replaced, nothing is added.

#### KV Cache effect

The tool list changes only when a review changes the registered set (a server change, a pin, or a revocation). Each such change alters the request header and invalidates the cached prefix once.

### Server instructions

#### What the model sees

The `### MCP server: <name>` prompt section appears only while the server's instructions match their pin (or policy `accept`). Policy `drop` and any rejected generation remove the section.

#### Token effect

Equal to or fewer than without the plugin.

#### KV Cache effect

Withdrawing or restoring the section changes the system prompt and invalidates the cached prefix once.

### Denied calls

#### What the model sees

A denied call returns an error result that names the server, what changed, and the exact commands, for example:

```text
MCP trust denied this call to server "files": its approved definition no longer matches the registered one (fields: description). The person can run `pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp diff files` to see what differs, then `pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp pin files` to approve it or `pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp revoke files` to keep it blocked. An allow rule never approves a server.
```

The variants are: approval removed from the lockfile; the server blocked or partly withheld (`it differs from its pin: 1 changed tool (fields: description)`, `it has no approved pin yet (3 added tools)`); the tool not registered through trust review; and an unreadable lockfile (the path, and that the file was not changed). The text contains the configured server name, counts, and field names from a fixed list; it never contains a tool name, description, or any other string a server supplied.

#### Token effect

One short tool error per denied call.

#### KV Cache effect

Append-only.

### Approval prompts

#### What the model sees

Nothing. The prompt text goes to the approval answerer (it lists tool names, which are server-supplied, so it is for the person only); tools accepted through it appear from the next step of the turn, because the first step's request was already assembled.

#### Token effect

None.

#### KV Cache effect

The next step's tool list changes once if the surface was accepted.

## Known Limitations

- A pin covers tool definitions and instructions, not the code that serves them. Use exact package versions in launch arguments; launch and artifact pinning are not implemented.
- The plugin cannot see duplicate JSON keys: the MCP SDK parses each message with `JSON.parse` before the tool list reaches the review.
- An mcp-client row without `inject: [mcpToolReview]` can register before this plugin loads. With `denyUnreviewedMcpTools: true` its calls are denied, but its descriptions reach the model until the row's next sync. The same setting denies `mcp__`-named tools registered by providers other than `dsh-mcp-client`; set it to `false` in a composition that has such providers.
- A change of negotiated protocol revision can alter generated fields such as `execution` and then reads as a changed tool. Re-pin after an SDK or server upgrade; the diff shows which fields changed.
- One lock entry covers every Agent scope that uses the same `serverName`.
- Live revocation relies on `fs.watch` of the lockfile's directory. Where the platform reports no file names (some network filesystems), a revocation applies at the server's next sync instead.
- Fail-closed lockfile: an unreadable lockfile (invalid JSON, wrong version, a field of the wrong type) blocks every MCP tool and is never rewritten; a UTF-8 byte order mark and CRLF line ends are accepted. Repair the file, or move it aside and pin each server again. Concurrent writers (the app and the `air-mcp` profile, or two CLI runs) take the sibling `.lock` file from `dsh-atomic-write`, wait up to `lockWaitMs`, and commit by atomic rename; a writer that times out reports the lock path. Writers on different machines sharing the file are unsupported.
- The first-use prompt shows tool names, not descriptions. Read the definitions with `diff <server>` before allowing when the server is not yours. A prompt-saved pin records `approvedBy: "prompt"` in the lockfile.
- The `air-mcp` profile connects only the server rows in its own patch. A server a project's `.mcp.json` adds (plan 01) is approved by the prompt; a later change to it can be accepted for one process at a time but not pinned from the command line. A shared server list is a follow-up.
- Windows: the code uses `node:path`, `node:fs`, and `process.execPath` only. It is written for native Windows but has been run only on Linux. A server started with `npx` may need `npx.cmd` in its row.
- One-shot acceptance and the prompt cap are per process. Each session adds one small entry to the audit writer's memory for the life of the process.
- If a registered tool is swapped out while one of its calls is in flight, that call settles on its own; only later calls are refused.
- Reloading this plugin restarts every mcp-client row that injects it, which restarts their stdio servers.
````

```markdown
## MCP trust

MCP servers register only tool definitions that match a reviewed lockfile (`$DSH_HOME/air/mcp-lock.json`). Both the `air` profile and the `air-mcp` command-line profile list `air/bundles/mcp-servers`, which holds the trust row. Every mcp-client row must declare `inject: [mcpToolReview]`; see [examples/mcp-filesystem.profile.cordis.patch.yml](examples/mcp-filesystem.profile.cordis.patch.yml).

The first turn after a server appears asks once whether to approve it; allowing it saves the pin. To read the definitions first, or to approve from a terminal, run `pnpm dsh --profile air-mcp diff <server>`, then `pin <server>`; `verify --all` is the CI check and `revoke <server>` removes approvals. Details: [packages/mcp-trust/README.md](packages/mcp-trust/README.md) and [packages/mcp-trust-cli/README.md](packages/mcp-trust-cli/README.md). `pnpm -C air exec tsx scripts/smoke-air-mcp.ts` checks the profile under an isolated harness home.
```

## Scenario coverage (spike 03 §13)

| # | Scenario | Where it is asserted |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Matching surface | `verdict.spec` "accepts a matching surface"; `loader.spec` "registers a matching surface"; `audit.spec` records verdict `approved` |
| 2 | Added unpinned | `verdict.spec`; `loader.spec` "withholds an added tool" (also the `process.jsonl` drift record) |
| 3 | Renamed | `verdict.spec` "treats a rename as a removal plus a withheld addition" |
| 4 | `inputSchema` widened | `canonical.spec`, `verdict.spec`; `loader.spec` quarantine test (zero tools, prompt section gone) |
| 5 | `additionalProperties: false` relaxed | `canonical.spec`, `verdict.spec` |
| 6 | Pinned tool missing | `verdict.spec` "accepts a missing pinned tool" |
| 7 | Key reorder, nested and `$defs` | `canonical.spec`; `loader.spec` "accepts reordered keys on the wire" |
| 8 | Subset pinned, honest server | `verdict.spec`, `loader.spec` "registers only allowed tools" |
| 9 | `browser_evaluate` appears | `verdict.spec` (denied and withheld variants); `loader.spec` (never registered) |
| 10 | Quarantine sticky across `list_changed` | `loader.spec` quarantine test (second change stays quarantined; restoring the surface recovers) |
| 11 | Description-only change | `canonical.spec`, `verdict.spec`, `loader.spec` |
| 12 | Instructions-only change | `verdict.spec`; `loader.spec` reconnect test |
| 13 | `readOnlyHint` flip | `canonical.spec`, `verdict.spec` |
| 14 | `outputSchema` widened | `canonical.spec` |
| 15 | Mutation during an in-flight call | `loader.spec` "lets an in-flight call settle" |
| 16 | Reconnect to a new surface | `loader.spec` reconnect test |
| 17 | Pagination split | `loader.spec` (`pageSize: 1`) |
| 18 | Invisible or homoglyph character | `canonical.spec`; rendering in `render.spec` |
| 19 | `1.0` versus `1` | `canonical.spec`; golden `tool` vector |
| 20 | Lone surrogate; duplicate raw name | `canonical.spec`, `verdict.spec`, `engine.spec`; duplicate name in upstream `review.spec` |
| 21 | External `$ref` | `canonical.spec` (hashed verbatim; the package contains no fetch code) |
| 22 | `override.description` | `verdict.spec`; `loader.spec` (model-visible schema) |
| 23 | Row without trust service | `row-inject.spec` (row pending); `engine.spec` (unreviewed `mcp__` call denied); `bundle.spec` (every AIR row injects) |
| 24 | TOFU first run, then change | `verdict.spec`, `engine.spec`, `loader.spec` |
| 25 | Headless `policy: never` | `approval.spec` with the real `ApprovalService` |
| 26 | Cross-language vector | `canonical.spec` against `jcs-golden.json` |
| 27 | HMR | `loader.spec` "trust plugin lifecycle"; `row-inject.spec` (service withdrawal and return) |
| + | Revoke in a running process | `engine.spec` (guard denies before re-sync); `loader.spec` (lockfile watch unregisters tools); `run.spec`, `startup.spec` (`revoke` command) |
| + | Explanation text on every denial and prompt (server, changed fields, `diff`/`pin` commands, no server-supplied text) | `engine.spec` guard tests; `approval.spec` `promptReason` tests |
| + | First-use enrollment; changed surface never pinned by a prompt; failed save falls back to one process | `engine.spec` one-shot tests |
| + | Unreadable lockfile: fail closed, path in the denial, recovery on repair; byte order mark and CRLF accepted; writers in separate processes serialize | `engine.spec`, `lockfile.spec` |
| + | `verify` exit codes | `run.spec`, `startup.spec`, `smoke-air-mcp.ts` |
| + | TTL re-verify | `engine.spec`, `loader.spec` |

## Follow-up plans

Moved out of this plan so it stays one reviewable slice. Each has a reason.

| Follow-up | Reason it is not here |
|---|---|
| `/mcp-trust` slash command (`status`, `diff`, `pin`, `revoke`) over the `mcpTrust` service, reusing `runTrustCli` with a string-collecting `CliIo` | Needed to pin a later change of a `.mcp.json` server without a second profile, but it adds a user-only command surface and its prompt-injection review (the command must not be model-invocable); the first-use prompt already covers the common case |
| One shared server list read by both the `air` and the `air-mcp` profile (an included file, or the profile patch itself) | The Loader's include semantics inside a profile patch are unverified; until then a server defined only in one profile is invisible to the other |
| Desktop shell panel listing `ctx.mcpTrust.servers()` with state and the commands to run (plan 07) | Plan 07 owns the UI; this plan exposes `observed(server).state` and `describe(server)` for it |
| Model-visible notice that a server is waiting for approval when no approval service exists | Needs an AIR-sourced injected message and its own tests; the log warning and the denial text cover the person today |
| Per-server trust level that skips plan 03's per-call ask | Plan 03 follow-up; the order of the two mechanisms is stated in the plugin README |
| Pinning the launch command, package version, and `protocolEra` (spike 03 §14) | Not required for tool-definition review; listed under Risks |

## Risks carried forward

- **Plan 01 (`.mcp.json` import) creates mcp-client rows.** Plan 01's `reviewTools` switch adds `inject: [mcpToolReview]` to every generated child, and this plan's `air` bundle sets it to `true`; `bundle.spec.ts` checks the static patches and the switch, and plan 01's own tests check the generated children.
- **Fork carry cost.** `syncTools` and `connectGeneration` are the most change-prone mcp-client code. The delta is confined to `review.ts`, one helper, and the insertion points listed in Task 1; `review.spec.ts` is a separate file so upstream test changes do not conflict.
- **`canonicalize` supply chain.** The version is exact (`5.1.0`); review the diff on any bump and rerun `canonical.spec.ts`. The I-JSON rejects stay in owned code.
- **Consent fatigue.** `maxPromptsPerServer` caps prompts per process; concurrent Agents are deduplicated by the engine's claim set.
- **Era-dependent digests.** Not mitigated in this plan (the lock entry does not record the negotiated protocol revision); the README states the re-pin procedure.

## How the RQ1 evaluation consumes these outputs

[Spike 06 §3](spikes/06-evaluation.md) defines the RQ1 drift corpus and its replay; plan 05 implements it. This plan supplies four inputs:

1. **Digest parity contract.** The corpus collector is Python and computes digests "by the same code as `@air/dsh-mcp-trust`" (spike 06 §3.1 step 5). The contract is `air/packages/mcp-trust/tests/fixtures/jcs-golden.json` together with the rules in Task 2: Python `rfc8785.dumps` over `{name, title?, description?, inputSchema, outputSchema?, annotations?, execution?}` with absent fields omitted, `sha256:` prefix, instructions hashed as raw UTF-8 text, and `surfaceDigest = sha256(JCS({instructions: <digest or null>, tools: {<rawName>: <toolDigest>}}))`. The evaluation project loads the same golden file in its own test, so a divergence between the Python collector and the TypeScript plugin fails on both sides.
2. **Lockfile as the pinned version.** For each consecutive-version transition in the corpus, version N's snapshot becomes a lock entry (the `servers.<name>` object of the format in Task 3) and version N+1's snapshot is the fetched surface. The "full surface" scheme of spike 06 §3.1 is `decide()` with the shipped defaults; "full surface + withhold" variants change only `onAdded` and `onChanged`. Because `decide()` is pure and takes `{ policy, entry, tools, instructions, acceptedOnce, now }`, the replay needs no model call, no MCP connection, and no harness process. The comparison schemes that are not AIR's (none; description-only) are implemented in the evaluation project against the same snapshots.
3. **`process.jsonl` for time-to-detect.** Spike 06 §3.1 measures detection latency (polling versus `list_changed`) in the harness with this plan's mutable fixture. Each `mcp/drift` line carries `time`; the driver records when it rewrote the fixture surface, and latency is the difference. Three conditions map to fixture fields: `list_changed` (default), TTL polling (`ttlMs` with `notify: false`, bounded by `minReverifyMs`), and reconnect (`restart: true` with a new `epoch`).
4. **`<sessionId>.jsonl` for per-episode attribution.** Spike 06 §5.2 lists the sidecar as the metrics source for "pinning verdicts, drift, quarantine". Each `mcp/surface` line gives the `surfaceDigest`, `verdict`, and `withheld` set that a session's turn ran against, keyed by `sessionId` and `turn`, so an AgentDojo or MCPTox episode (RQ2 arms with and without pinning) can be joined to the tool surface the model saw without adding a session event type. False-alert counts for RQ1 come from `verdict` values other than `approved` on benign transitions; MCPTox's added-tool poisoning (spike 06 finding 6) appears as `mcp/drift` with a non-empty `added` list.

The evaluation run sets `lockfile` and `auditDir` per run through an arm patch (both are plain `Config` fields), so runs do not share pins or audit files and nothing under `~/.dsh` is read.

## Self-Review

- **Spec coverage.** Spike 03 §1 (hook, service rather than waterfall, instructions in the same verdict, fetch failure versus drift, HMR) is Task 1 plus `row-inject.spec.ts`; §2 (guard through `ctx.tools.guard`) is Task 5; §3 (sidecar audit, no session events) is Task 6; §4 (lockfile location, locked atomic write, watch, format) is Task 3 and the bundle in Task 9; §5 (profile command line) is Task 8; §6 (approval at `agent/pre-step` step 1) is Task 7; §7 (`canonicalize` 5.1.0 behind an I-JSON wrapper, digest inputs, Python golden) is Task 2; §8 (mutable fixture) is Task 0; §9 (`ttlMs` timers) is Task 5; §10 (interfaces) is `types.ts` and `index.ts`; §11 (packages) is the File Structure; §12 (T0–T9) is the task order; §13 is the scenario table above. Research note 04 §3.2's `launch` block and `capability` field, and §3.8, are out of scope by the spike's own §14.
- **Gaps stated, not hidden.** The ten deviations are listed at the top with reasons, the Revision log lists what changed on 2026-10-08 and what was run, and the Follow-up plans table lists what moved out. The `protocolEra` mitigation of spike 03 §14 is not implemented and is listed under risks and in the README.
- **Type consistency.** `McpToolReviewRequest`/`McpToolReviewVerdict` (Task 1) are the parameter and return types of `TrustEngine.review` (Task 5). `Decision<T>` fields (`accepted`, `instructions`, `diff`, `state`, `action`, `withheld`, `denied`, `surfaceDigest`, `digests`, `tofu`) are the ones `engine.ts` reads. `ObservedSurface` carries `action`, `withheld`, and `pinnedDigest`, which `audit.ts` and `render.ts` read. `PendingSurface` (`serverName`, `surface`, `resync`) is produced by `takePromptable` and consumed by `settlePrompt` and `createPreStep`. `DriftRecord` is the engine's drift listener payload and the audit `mcp/drift` data. `McpTrust` method names (`observed`, `servers`, `describe`, `pin`, `revoke`, `verify`) match between `types.ts`, `engine.ts`, `run.ts`, and both CLI test fakes; `pin`'s `approvedBy` accepts `'prompt'` in `types.ts`, `engine.ts`, and `lockfile.ts` (`ApprovedBy`). `EngineConfig` and `Config` both carry `cliCommand` and `enrollOnApproval`, and `settlePrompt` returns a promise in the engine, its test, the `PreStepDeps` pick, and the listener. `boot()` options (`surface`, `rows`, `reviewed`) match every caller.
- **Placeholders.** None: every code step shows the complete file or the exact inserted lines, and every run step states the command and the expected result.
